    0. SUMMARY
    - lc0a lost because this setup had no edge. Its maker quotes were picked off through a VPN path running ~1.4-2.3x the latency floor, and machine stalls made it worse.
    - It kept trading for three reasons:
      - nothing carried the environment into the room;
      - the room had no channel for an infrastructure blocker;
      - money kept flowing past every point where it could have stopped.
    - Built:
      - a launch preflight;
      - needs/ escalation, with an owed answer from the owners of blocked claims and a question on each proposal that names any needs/ already open (855700f0; see C2 for the probed build);
      - machine-stall lines in the hub;
      - netcheck;
      - the escalation scorer.
    - After the change, on the same brief, the environment reached the room by second 20-23, and an owner item was filed within 6 min in 2/2 rooms (1 of them unprompted). Before: 0/2.
    - On a held-out brief, both arms filed a needs/. That result is a ceiling, because the brief itself scopes paid feeds into needs/. Only the after arm named eligibility/geoblock, at 0.4 min; the before arm never mentioned it.
    - The owner's next step is a $12/mo read-only recorder in London, not a trading box.
    
    A. DIAGNOSIS
    
    MARKET (seat 3; evidence/market-side, takers-timing, item3-network-path)
    - A1: adverse selection, not fees.
      - Fees were $2.57 on ~$2,021 notional. 512 of the 610 fills were maker fills.
      - Crypto-maker lost $32.03 on the 189 of its 257 fills that can be priced. The 90% CI is [-60, -4], and seat 5's rerun agrees.
    - A2: latency was the mechanism. Guarded quotes did no better.
      - The one-way path is ~150-245 ms, depending on method, against a ~107 ms floor.
      - 150-170 ms is item3's model. 245 ms is netcheck's measured sum and is an upper bound.
    - A3: the path ran through NordVPN via Zurich, because GB is close-only.
    - A4, LONDON: what a London server would change.
      - It saves about 45-140 ms at the median (about 50 on item3's model, up to ~140 on netcheck's upper-bound sum) and removes all of the machine-made tail, because the bot leaves the laptop.
      - Takers' wave arrives ~250 ms after a Binance move. Guard cancels landed at ~350 ms from the laptop (13 of 16 filled anyway). From London they are predicted, not measured, to land at ~125-150 ms.
      - It does not fix the 30 s quiet-fill drift, the fees, the colo makers, or the lack of any paper edge.
      - A London box can only record, since GB is close-only. Ireland is handled by the owner outside this room (inputs/owner-4).
    
    MACHINE (seat 4; evidence/machine)
    - M1: at 19:30-19:39 the whole machine starved. Hub loop lag p50 went from 21 ms to 14.4 s.
    - M2: the median path alone ruled out a laptop maker. The stall made the tail (p99 7.3 s vs 0.77 s outside it). While the stall lasted it roughly doubled the loss per fill: $0.28 vs $0.13 (one evening, no CI).
    - M3: the chair measured load 31 and swap at 7/8 GB, but the room never heard.
    - M4: from GB the VPN is required. Fixing the path and fixing the machine are two separate needs.

    - Q1, noticing a blocker (seat 5; decision/blocker-detection PRODUCTS, sources/grokbot-re-approvals-pause, sources/conductor-re-waiting).
      - Neither product classifies chat. A blocker is a typed waiting state raised at an action boundary or by a rule a person wrote, with a named reason, and a person clears it. That backs C2's ask-at-the-proposal design over word lists.
      - The one structural signal they add is spend continuing while nobody reads. In lc0a the chair was silent for 58 min (#251 to #612) while trading went on.
      - What remains: idea/unattended-live-guard, a hub line after N min with no human in a declared live room. At 20 min it would have fired at 20:18. It would likely have drawn a "keep" from the owner, who was following out of band, so it records a decision rather than stopping anything. Its auto-hold step needs the owner's word. Not built.
    - Q2, the pause (seat 4; decision/pause, sources/grokbot-pause-rules). In both products a pause is a gate at the point of action, set beforehand by a person, and only a person lifts it. Our needs/ + blocks: is advisory and aimed at agents, and lc0a's bots and gateway read nothing in the room. What remains:
      - R1: the gateway refuses an order while an open need blocks its claim; only the owner or chair clears it.
      - R2: a loss past the floor files that need itself.
      - R3: document that hold/<room> holds the conclusion, not the work.
    - Q3, carry-forward (seat 1; decision/carry-forward, sources/grokbot-memory-facts, sources/conductor-setup-and-scopes).
      - Both products scope knowledge to the team or repo, not to a directory, and show what a new workspace inherits.
      - Environment facts: re-measure instead of remembering. C1/C4 already do this, and it matches Conductor's per-workspace setup script.
      - Strategy negatives: id-tagged records in a kit file shipped via --resources (idea/known-negatives; kit is read-only here).
    - Q4, loss floor (seat 3): in E.
    - Built from this brainstorm: nothing. Each candidate either needs the gateway or kit (read-only here) or lacks evidence that it would have changed lc0a.
    
    F. NOT SHOWN
    - Any effect at n>1.
    - A live chair.
    - London latency (predicted only).
    - A grouped run with a preflight.
    - Whether a WARN-only case escalates.
    - The stall line on a room like lc0a.
    - 855700f0 in a live room. It is merged and its regression passes at 5bf8c92e, but no probe ran on it.

#53 [2026-10-23T07:36:16.612Z] claude-opus-5-5-5 (chat): evidence/rules-that-kept-trading is up. Headlines: (1) the owner asked the chair about a VPS at 19:02 and "Let's get a server close to the exchange then?" at 20:00, both in the chair session, before the 20:24 Dublin question elsewhere. At 19:02 the chair had measured NordVPN and load 31, at 19:34 7/8 GB swap, and at 20:04 that the owner is in the UK (close-only). The room heard none of it: 0 room messages mention VPN, load or geoblock. (2) The chair advised "don't stop yet, but cap it" at 20:29. (3) Money asks never touched needs/*: the chair granted 5 top-ups in-room (#64 #80 #150 #164), one to crypto-maker after its -$30. needs/* covered only account/feed/key/money, so an infra blocker had no channel. (4) The brief made "how fast you really are from this laptop" the thing under study, so #235 read as a result, not a blocker. Also, the hub has no needs/ concept at all (grep src: 0 hits). BUILD I'd take: claim/needs-escalation, a reserved needs/<name> board prefix. The hub would push it to the chair and humans, stamp telemetry (need_filed, minutes since room open and since the seat first cited the constraint), list open needs in room_status and the report, plus a minimal.md line: "if the setup blocks or invalidates what you're testing, stop that work, write needs/<name>, tell the chair; don't route around it". @claude-opus-5-5-2 you planned a blocker/pause primitive in your opening. Should we make this one team (I build the hub prefix and telemetry, you the pause semantics and benchmark), or do you want it?

#55 [2026-10-23T07:36:35.588Z] claude-opus-5-5-1 (chat): evidence/earlier-rooms is up. Headline: the link problem was found and fixed on 13 Oct. In mrw4-crypto (#54, #167, #180) the room saw a tether plus NordVPN at 196 ms RTT with 5% loss, and Polymarket's "1013 slow consumer". The owner turned the VPN off at 14:30, and after that RTT was 25 ms with ~0 book lag (#253). That fact reached RULEBOOK.md:72 and the chair's memory, which says "measure on a machine with its own clean link". It was trimmed on the way into the live room: live_room/resources.md:33-34 keeps "0.05 s on a clean link" without the VPN clause. launch.sh has no network check, --no-carry turned seat memory off, and lc0a mentions VPN 0 times. That fits seat 5's #53: the chair knew at 19:02 and the room never heard it. Separately, every strategy class lc0a funded had already FAILED on paper, for the same latency mechanism.
    @claude-opus-5-5-4 your geoblock point changes the remedy. Turning the VPN off was fine for reading data, but from GB the API is close-only. A trading room therefore needs a non-GB exit with low RTT, which is the honest case for a Dublin box. I'll draft that needs/ entry with @claude-opus-5-5-3 unless seat 3 has it.
    BUILD: claim/launch-preflight. Before seats start, swarm.ts runs the environment checks a kit declares (VPN/default route, RTT and loss to clob/ws, clock offset, load and swap). It records a preflight event in the room log and posts preflight/env on the board, and on failure it either refuses or flags the launch. It sits beside seat 4's mid-run stall signal and seat 5's needs/ prefix: the preflight checks before the room starts, the stall signal watches during it, needs/ is how a seat escalates. #79 [2026-10-23T07:38:00.951Z] claude-opus-5-5-3 (chat): evidence/item3-network-path is up. Headlines:
    (1) The VPN, the Zurich exit, Polymarket in eu-west-2 behind Cloudflare (ZRH POP), and Binance's stream in AWS Tokyo are all confirmed by measurement and primary sources.
    (2) A trap for anyone measuring latency here: curl time_connect is fake through this VPN. A Tokyo IP "connects" in 30 ms. Use the TLS handshake or the age of ws messages instead.
    (3) A London-adjacent box saves roughly 50 ms at the median (about 150-170 ms down to the ~107 ms Tokyo-to-London floor). Its real win is removing the multi-second tails that seat 4 measured. It does not touch the 0.07·p(1-p) taker fee.
    (4) The session's "Dublin is blocked" came from the website geoblock check, which the docs say IS blocked for IE while the API is not. Neither claim is established for API orders.
    (5) The owner lives in GB. A server abroad doesn't change that, and Polymarket is reportedly blocking VPNs. Every server needs/* entry carries that as risk #1.
    @claude-opus-5-5-1, I'll write the install list (B: needs/* plus a ranking) under claim/install-list-and-netcheck. Please don't draft the Dublin entry separately; add anything you have to it. I'll also build the netcheck tool in tools/ so your launch-preflight can call it as the kit's declared check. Does that split work for you?
    @claude-opus-5-5-4, yes on the interpretation: your stalls are the tail, my path numbers are the median.

I own the launcher side: `swarm --preflight "<cmd>" [--preflight-strict]`. Please make netcheck print this, one JSON object per line on stdout (other lines are ignored):
    {"check":"vpn-route","status":"ok|warn|fail","value":"utun7 via 10.5.0.2","why":"one line: what it means for the room","fix":"optional: what the owner would do"}
    Exit 0 even when checks fail. Status decides; a crash or no lines counts as a preflight fail. The launcher posts the result as resource/preflight-environment-at-launch, puts a FAIL/WARN block at the top of every seat's brief, writes it to result.json and the report, and refuses the launch under --preflight-strict. Nice to have: a combined "trading-path" check, e.g. fail with value "exit CH allowed, TLS p50 160 ms", since "allowed exit AND low RTT" is the thing a live room needs. Thanks for the time_connect trap; I'll write the fixture against TLS numbers.

#645 [2026-10-23T09:25:03.060Z] claude-opus-5-5-1 (proposal): PROPOSAL prop_50669bf0: FINAL (seat 1, assembled from the wip/final-* sections, bench/escalation, probe/*, evidence/*. Gates per decision/chair-final-assembly:
    - the after-arm and the held-out pair have landed;
    - inputs/owner-3 is answered by decision/blocker-detection;
    - integration dea483aa builds, prints SMOKE OK and passes npm test 326/326.
    Every number below the summary has a non-author check: verify/market-side-diagnosis, verify/install-list-and-netcheck, verify/item3-separate-session-claims, evidence/verifier-spotchecks, evidence/verifier-p5-recount and review/*. The sources/* the hub listed as unexamined are background reading, not claims this text rests on.)
    
    0. SUMMARY
    - lc0a lost because this setup had no edge. Its maker quotes were picked off through a VPN path running ~1.4-2.3x the latency floor, and machine stalls made it worse.
    - It kept trading for three reasons:
      - nothing carried the environment into the room;
      - the room had no channel for an infrastructure blocker;
      - money kept flowing past every point where it could have stopped.
    - Built:
      - a launch preflight;
      - needs/ escalation, with an owed answer from the owners of blocked claims and a question on every proposal;
      - machine-stall lines in the hub;
      - netcheck;
      - the escalation scorer.
    - After the change, on the same brief, the environment reached the room by second 20-23, and an owner item was filed within 6 min in 2/2 rooms (1 of them unprompted). Before: 0/2.
    - On a held-out brief, both arms filed a needs/. That result is a ceiling, because the brief itself scopes paid feeds into needs/. Only the after arm named eligibility/geoblock, at 0.4 min; the before arm never mentioned it.
    - The owner's next step is a $12/mo read-only recorder in London, not a trading box.
    
    A. DIAGNOSIS
    
    MARKET (seat 3; evidence/market-side, takers-timing, item3-network-path)
    - A1: adverse selection, not fees.
      - Fees were $2.57 on ~$2,021 notional. 512 of the 610 fills were maker fills.
      - Crypto-maker lost $32.03 on the 189 of its 257 fills that can be priced. The 90% CI is [-60, -4], and seat 5's rerun agrees.
    - A2: latency was the mechanism. Guarded quotes did no better.
      - The one-way path is ~150-245 ms, depending on method, against a ~107 ms floor.
      - 150-170 ms is item3's model. 245 ms is netcheck's measured sum and is an upper bound.
    - A3: the path ran through NordVPN via Zurich, because GB is close-only.
    - A4, LONDON: what a London server would change.
      - It saves about 50 ms at the median and removes all of the machine-made tail, because the bot leaves the laptop.
      - Takers' wave arrives ~250 ms after a Binance move. Guard cancels landed at ~350 ms from the laptop (13 of 16 filled anyway). From London they are predicted, not measured, to land at ~125-150 ms.
      - It does not fix the 30 s quiet-fill drift, the fees, the colo makers, or the lack of any paper edge.
      - A London box can only record, since GB is close-only. Dublin is restricted (GRAI, 21 Jul).
    
    MACHINE (seat 4; evidence/machine)
    - M1: at 19:30-19:39 the whole machine starved. Hub loop lag p50 went from 21 ms to 14.4 s.
    - M2: the median path alone ruled out a laptop maker. The stall made the tail (p99 7.3 s vs 0.77 s outside it). While the stall lasted it roughly doubled the loss per fill: $0.28 vs $0.13 (one evening, no CI).
    - M3: the chair measured load 31 and swap at 7/8 GB, but the room never heard.
    - M4: from GB the VPN is required. Fixing the path and fixing the machine are two separate needs.
    
    ROOM PROCESS (seat 1; wip/final-section-A-process)
    - P1: the VPN fact was found on 13 Oct but was trimmed out on its way into lc0a. --no-carry hid the memory note.
    - P2: no room measured its environment first. The brief made the laptop the object of study.
    - P3: 9 owner infrastructure questions and the chair's measurements stayed out of band; 0 were relayed.
    - P4: needs/ covered only money and accounts, so 0 needs/ were filed. Sibling rooms lost ~$31-40 more over 61 min. $31 is the gap between ledger marks; $39.64 is attribution by fill time.
    - P5: the chair topped up budgets during losses, and there was no loss floor.
    - P6: every funded strategy class had already failed on paper.
    - P7: no prompt said to pause.
    
    B. OWNER INSTALL LIST, ranked (decision/install-ranking; one needs/* entry each)
    1. london-readonly-recorder: Lightsail London, $12/mo, no orders.
    2. ask-polymarket-eligibility: free.
    3. trading-box-allowed-country: conditional on 1 and 2. Stockholm at $12-24, or Hetzner Helsinki, which is listed from €5.99 excl. VAT but was unavailable at the verifier's fetch. Check stock and price before buying.
    4. swarm-host: ~$84/mo, optional.
    5. polymarket-colo: unpriced, needs KYC; conditional on 1.
    
    C. BUILT (every commit reviewed by a non-author; integration branch swarm/swarm-073112-zcd7/integration at dea483aa: 27 reviewed commits in 15 merges; build OK, npm test OK (326 commands), SMOKE OK; tools integration c253b5b, netcheck 12/12)
    
    C1 launch preflight (seat 1; result/launch-preflight)
    - Commits: 53d25a5d, 821781c3, c4a61872, a6560403, 9c907aaf. Answers P1 and P2.
    - Telemetry: a room-log preflight event {fails, warns, ok}, result.json, the report, and the launch log, which prints each FAIL/WARN with its owner fix.
    - Before: 0 VPN mentions in lc0a and in esc-before-1/2.
    - After: 3/3 openings cited the FAILs at second 20 in esc-after-1. In a dev room (n=1), a needs/ was filed at 17 s. In heldout-after the preflight showed 7 FAIL / 2 WARN, and needs/ask-polymarket-eligibility followed 0.4 min after open.
    - --preflight-strict would have refused lc0a's launch.
    
    C2 needs/ escalation (seat 5; wip/final-section-C-needs)
    - Commits: 20024008; aef07b4d, which removed the word-list hints after held-out recall of 1/6 for both detectors (inputs/owner-3); edb75ca3, the owner ask; d8cd2091, the room question. Answers P4 and P7.
    - Telemetry: need_filed, need_cleared, need_asked, and run-metrics needs {filed, cleared, blocks, asked, askedThenFiled, asksOfOwners, ownersAnswered}. ownersAnswered counts replies, not pauses.
    - Before: 0 needs/ in lc0a and esc-before-1/2.
    - After: dev rooms filed at 1.0 min (dev room, n=1 each).
    - The owner ask was used live once, in heldout-after. needs/score-feed (blocks: claim/exchange-ts) led to the hub's ask (#29). The owner answered 90 s later (#36) and kept working, with a reason. That is n=1, and it was an answer, not a pause.
    - KNOWN LIMIT: an open needs/* silences the proposal question, so in esc-after-2 the London box stayed in prose with no need filed.
      - The fix, which asks anyway and names the open needs, is 855700f0. It is reviewed (review/855700f0) but not merged: it was held out so the probed build and the tested build stay the same.
      - Seat 5 owns it for the next room (claim/ask-despite-open-need).
    
    C3 machine-stall lines (seat 4; wip/final-section-C-machine)
    - Commits: 5d32f929..a53cd1fe; reviews review/5d32f929, 591fbbab, 40f5a4c0. Answers M3 and the mid-run half of P2.
    - Telemetry: room-log events machine (the machine a room opened on) and machine_stall start|end, plus run-metrics machine {stalls, stalled_s, worst_ms}.
    - Probes: machine-stall-1 (after, $3.87) and machine-stall-0 (before, $4.39). No measured effect, with n=1 per arm and unmatched arms.
    - No false alarms: esc-after-1, esc-after-2 and heldout-after each recorded 0 machine_stall events, with max hub loop lag of 159, 47 and 33 ms.
    - The effect on a room like lc0a is unmeasured (idea/stall-line-unprimed-room).
    
    C4 netcheck and analysis (seat 3, tools repo; branches claude-opus-5-5-3/netcheck and /analysis)
    - Netcheck commits: 178fc4a, 40bc027, 8781d17, f1a98a9. f1a98a9 makes the IE/NL reason cite the regulator; its review is review/f1a98a9.
    - Analysis commit: 1c4553c (pnl.py, sniper_lag.py).
    - Reviews: review/178fc4a, 40bc027, 8781d17, f1a98a9, 1c4553c, and verify/install-list-and-netcheck. Answers A2, A3 and P2.
    - Telemetry: one JSON line per check, carried in the preflight event and result.json. The analysis scripts run offline and add no telemetry.
    - Not shown: a run on a London or allowed-country box.
    
    C5 escalation scorer and chair-relay rule (seat 2)
    - Commits: 1fd346c5, 397fee31, 313fa18f, 7a99dffd, e91f2bba; reviews review/1fd346c5, 313fa18f, 7a99dffd, e91f2bba.
    - scripts/escalation-bench.ts reads room logs offline and is the instrument for D. Per room it reports:
      - whether a preflight ran before the first experiment;
      - the machine reading;
      - the first evidence;
      - minutes from evidence to escalation;
      - restricted-place needs/;
      - any outside stop;
      - owner step-ins.
    - On lc0a it reports no preflight and 61 min with nothing escalated.
    - Its evidence detector is a pattern list, and found the evidence in 1 of the 2 held-out rooms. The hand-read seq is therefore the primary number.
    - The chair-relay rule is unmeasured, because no probe had a live chair.
    
    D. BENCHMARK (bench/escalation; 10/10 probes used, $6-8 each)
    - Same brief:
      - lc0a, esc-before-1 and esc-before-2: no preflight, no escalation, VPN never mentioned.
      - After: 2/2 filed an owner-only needs/, at 3.8 and 5.8 min after the evidence. esc-after-1 (3145b336) filed only after the verifier's challenge. esc-after-2 (45aa1137) filed with no challenge and no hint.
      - Both after rooms named the VPN by second 20-23. Neither filed a trading-box need.
      - Owner step-ins were 0 by construction.
    - Held-out brief (paper in-play sports taker; nobody tuned against it):
      - heldout-before (e91f2bba, no preflight): needs/point-feed ($150/mo) was filed unprompted 2.0 min after the evidence; open to escalation 10.6 min; 22 min; $7.30; geoblock/eligibility never mentioned.
      - heldout-after (dea483aa, --preflight netcheck): needs/ask-polymarket-eligibility at 0.2 min after the evidence and 0.4 min after open, ranked first; 14 min; $6.45; 0 step-ins; 0 stalls.
      - This pair is a CEILING on "filed a needs/": the held-out brief scopes paid feeds into needs/. Also, the after arm's "evidence" is the preflight restated, so it measures carrying a known constraint, not discovering one.
      - The restricted-place flag on needs/clean-london-box was a hand-checked false positive: that box is a recorder.
    
    E. OWNER DECISIONS (wip/final-section-owner-decisions; nothing built)
    - P5 loss floor: options a-d, sourced from SEC 15c3-5 and RTS 6.
    - By fill attribution, a floor at 20:29 would have avoided ~-$19, and one at 20:08 ~-$25.
    - Kit lines: declare a preflight (decision/kit-preflight-line); idea/known-negatives (needs the kit, which is read-only to this room).
    
    F. NOT SHOWN
    - Any effect at n>1.
    - A live chair.
    - London latency (predicted only).
    - A grouped run with a preflight.
    - Whether a WARN-only case escalates.
    - The stall line on a room like lc0a.
    - 855700f0 in a room.
